        Wait, if Black King is at g4, the square g2 is no longer attacked by the Black King.
        So White King at h1 could move to g2.
        Is that good for White? Yes, it helps him escape the corner.
        So maybe staying at f2, f3, or f4 is better to keep the White King trapped?
        If Black moves `g3f2`, the White King is still blocked by the pawn at h2 and the Black King controls g3? No, if Black moves to f2, he doesn't control g3 anymore. He controls g1, g2, g3?
        Wait, from f2, the king attacks: e1, e2, e3, f1, f3, g1, g2, g3.
        So if the Black king is at f2, he attacks g1 and g2.
        This means the White King at h1 is completely trapped! He can't move to g1 or g2.
        This is very important. If the White King is trapped, it's easier to aim for a stalemate.

        Let's check `g3f3`:
        From f3, the king attacks: e2, e3, e4, f2, f4, g2, g3, g4.
        He attacks g2, but NOT g1.
        So the White King could move to g1.

        Let's check `g3f4`:
        From f4, the king attacks: e3, e4, e5, f3, f5, g3, g4, g5.
        He doesn't attack g1 or g2.
        So the White King could move to g1 and g2.

        Let's check `g3g4`:
        From g4, the king attacks: f3, f4, f5, g3, g5, h3, h4, h5.
        He doesn't attack g1 or g2.
        So the White King could move to g1 and g2.

        Conclusion: `g3f2` is the only move that keeps the White King completely trapped in the corner (since f2 attacks both g1 and g2).

        If the White King is trapped, and White promotes the pawn, can Black force a stalemate?
        It's hard, but it's the only chance.

        Therefore, `g3f2` is the most strategic move.
